\begin{isamarkuptext}%
The values (and as a result the semantics) are parametrized by the carrier type 'a for the 
abstract values (values that have a type constructed via type constructors)
TODO: explain Map Values%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%

\begin{isamarkuptext}%
We differentiate between DeBruijn variables (used for bound variales) and named variables. When we open 
a term in the semantics, we do not change the bound variable constructors (i.e., we treat bound 
variables as in the pure DeBruijn case). Opening a term happens for example, when one evaluates a
universal quantifier (recursing over the body of the quantifier without the quantifier itself is
called "opening"). As a result, we need a separate state in the semantics to track the bound variables 
that become temporarily free in the semantics.

We did not pick a pure DeBruijn approach, since it becomes a bit unnatural to differentiate local
and global variables. Moreover, we did not pick a locally nameless approach, since generating proofs
becomes cumbersome due to the free variable constraints when opening terms.%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%

\begin{isamarkuptext}%
\isa{\isaconst{old{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}global{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}state}} stores the global state (global variable mapping) 
as it was at the beginning of the procedure (required for old expressions).
\isa{\isaconst{global{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}state}} stores the current global variable mapping.
\isa{\isaconst{local{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}state}} stores the current parameter/local variable/return variable mapping.
\isa{\isaconst{binder{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}state}} tracks the bound variables that become free (i.e., it is empty before and
after a program statement in practice).%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%

declarations (parameters, local variables, return variables)%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%

\begin{isamarkuptext}%
Function interpretation:
  A Boogie function is semantically represented by an Isabelle function that takes as parameters the 
  instantiated type parameters and the argument values%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%
\isakeywordONE{type{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}synonym}\isamarkupfalse%
\ {\isacharparenleft}{\kern0pt}{\isacharprime}{\kern0pt}a{\isacharcomma}{\kern0pt}\ {\isacharprime}{\kern0pt}m{\isacharparenright}{\kern0pt}\ fun{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}repr\ {\isacharequal}{\kern0pt}\ {\isachardoublequoteopen}ty\ list\ {\isasymRightarrow}\ {\isacharparenleft}{\kern0pt}{\isacharprime}{\kern0pt}a{\isacharcomma}{\kern0pt}\ {\isacharprime}{\kern0pt}m{\isacharparenright}{\kern0pt}\ val\ list\ {\isasymrightharpoonup}\ {\isacharparenleft}{\kern0pt}{\isacharprime}{\kern0pt}a{\isacharcomma}{\kern0pt}\ {\isacharprime}{\kern0pt}m{\isacharparenright}{\kern0pt}\ val{\isachardoublequoteclose}\isanewline
\isakeywordONE{type{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}synonym}\isamarkupfalse%

\begin{isamarkuptext}%
Type interpretation:
Each value of the abstract carrier type must be mapped to a corresponding type (constructed via a
type constructor).%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%

\begin{isamarkuptext}%
The following condition specifies what must hold for the list of constants with a unique modifier.
      The condition states that all corresponding values in the state must be distinct. Note that constants
      without unique modifiers may have values that clash with unique constants, which is consistent with the 
      verification condition generated by Boogie (status 21.10.2023).
      Note that the verification condition only forces distinctness between values of unique constants of 
      the \emph{same} type. Here, we force distinctness between values of all unique constants. These two
      definitions are equivalent, since values of different types are distinct in Boogie by default
      (every value can have only one type as reflected by the function \isa{\isaconst{type{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}of{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}val}}).%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%

\begin{isamarkuptext}%
\isa{\isaconst{proc{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}body{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}satisfies{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}spec}} states when a procedure's CFG is correct w.r.t. postconditions \isa{\isafree{posts}}

add an additional assumption here (proof generation does not support where-clauses currently).%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%

\begin{isamarkuptext}%
\isa{\isafree{proc{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}is{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}correctfun{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}decls}\ \isafree{constants}\ \isafree{unique{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}consts}\ \isafree{global{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}vars}\ \isafree{axioms}\ \isafree{procproc{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}body{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}satisfies{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}spec{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}general}} gives the definition
that a procedure \isa{\isafree{proc}} is correct w.r.t. the type interpretation \isa{\isafree{A}} the function declarations \isa{fun{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}decls}, 
constants \isa{\isafree{constants}}, global variables \isa{\isafree{global{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}vars}} and Boogie axioms \isa{\isafree{axioms}}. 
\isa{\isafree{unique{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}consts}} denotes the list of constants with a unique modifier.

Since the current proof generation does not support procedure calls yet, we just instantiate the
procedure context to the empty list here. 

In our certificates, we prove 
\isa{{\isasymAnd}\isabound{A}{\isachardot}{\kern0pt}\ \isafree{proc{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}is{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}correctfun{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}decls}\ \isafree{constants}\ \isafree{unique{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}consts}\ \isafree{global{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}vars}\ \isafree{axioms}\ \isafree{procproc{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}body{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}satisfies{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}spec{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}general}}.
That is, we prove procedure correctness for every type interpretation (\<And> is a universal quantifier at 
the meta level). Note that for certain type interpretations procedure correctness is trivial (see
the definition of \isa{\isaconst{proc{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}is{\isacharunderscore}{\kern0pt}correct}}).%
\end{isamarkuptext}\isamarkuptrue%
